1→"""Helper utilities for plan triage workflow."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→from collections import defaultdict
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     9→from desloppify.engine.plan import (
    10→    TRIAGE_IDS,
    11→    TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS,
    12→    purge_ids,
    13→    review_issue_snapshot_hash,
    14→)
    15→from desloppify.state import utc_now
    16→
    17→from .services import TriageServices, default_triage_services
    18→
    19→_STAGE_ORDER = ["observe", "reflect", "organize", "enrich"]
    20→
    21→
    22→def has_triage_in_queue(plan: dict) -> bool:
    23→    """Check if any triage stage ID is in the queue."""
    24→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    25→    return bool(order & TRIAGE_IDS)
    26→
    27→def inject_triage_stages(plan: dict) -> None:
    28→    """Inject all 4 triage stage IDs into the queue (fresh start)."""
    29→    order: list[str] = plan.setdefault("queue_order", [])
    30→    existing = set(order)
    31→    for sid in TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS:
    32→        if sid not in existing:
    33→            order.insert(0 if sid == TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS[0] else len(order), sid)
    34→    # Re-insert in correct order at front
    35→    for sid in reversed(TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS):
    36→        if sid in order:
    37→            order.remove(sid)
    38→    insert_at = 0
    39→    for sid in TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS:
    40→        order.insert(insert_at, sid)
    41→        insert_at += 1
    42→
    43→def purge_triage_stage(plan: dict, stage_name: str) -> None:
    44→    """Purge a single triage stage ID from the queue."""
    45→    sid = f"triage::{stage_name}"
    46→    purge_ids(plan, [sid])
    47→
    48→def cascade_clear_later_confirmations(stages: dict, from_stage: str) -> list[str]:
    49→    """Clear confirmed_at/confirmed_text on stages AFTER *from_stage*. Returns cleared names."""
    50→    try:
    51→        idx = _STAGE_ORDER.index(from_stage)
    52→    except ValueError:
    53→        return []
    54→    cleared: list[str] = []
    55→    for later in _STAGE_ORDER[idx + 1:]:
    56→        if later in stages and stages[later].get("confirmed_at"):
    57→            stages[later].pop("confirmed_at", None)
    58→            stages[later].pop("confirmed_text", None)
    59→            cleared.append(later)
    60→    return cleared
    61→
    62→def print_cascade_clear_feedback(cleared: list[str], stages: dict) -> None:
    63→    """Print yellow cascade-clear message with next-step guidance."""
    64→    if not cleared:
    65→        return
    66→    print(colorize(f"  Cleared confirmations on: {', '.join(cleared)}", "yellow"))
    67→    next_unconfirmed = next(
    68→        (s for s in _STAGE_ORDER if s in stages and not stages[s].get("confirmed_at")),
    69→        None,
    70→    )
    71→    if next_unconfirmed:
    72→        print(colorize(
    73→            f"  Re-confirm with: desloppify plan triage --confirm {next_unconfirmed}",
    74→            "dim",
    75→        ))
    76→
    77→def observe_dimension_breakdown(si) -> tuple[dict[str, int], list[str]]:
    78→    """Count issues per dimension from a TriageInput. Returns (by_dim, sorted_dim_names)."""
    79→    by_dim: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
    80→    for _fid, f in si.open_issues.items():
    81→        detail = f.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(f.get("detail"), dict) else {}
    82→        dim = detail.get("dimension", "unknown")
    83→        by_dim[dim] += 1
    84→    dim_names = sorted(by_dim, key=lambda d: (-by_dim[d], d))
    85→    return dict(by_dim), dim_names
    86→
    87→def open_review_ids_from_state(state: dict) -> set[str]:
    88→    """Return IDs of all open review/concerns issues in state."""
    89→    return {
    90→        fid for fid, f in state.get("issues", {}).items()
    91→        if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
    92→    }
    93→
    94→def triage_coverage(
    95→    plan: dict,
    96→    open_review_ids: set[str] | None = None,
    97→) -> tuple[int, int, dict]:
    98→    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for review issues in triage.
    99→
   100→    When *open_review_ids* is provided, use it as the full set of review
   101→    issues (from state) instead of falling back to queue_order.
   102→    """
   103→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   104→    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
   105→    for c in clusters.values():
   106→        all_cluster_ids.update(c.get("issue_ids", []))
   107→    if open_review_ids is not None:
   108→        review_ids = list(open_review_ids)
   109→    else:
   110→        review_ids = [
   111→            fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", [])
   112→            if not fid.startswith("triage::") and not fid.startswith("workflow::") and (fid.startswith("review::") or fid.startswith("concerns::"))
   113→        ]
   114→    organized = sum(1 for fid in review_ids if fid in all_cluster_ids)
   115→    return organized, len(review_ids), clusters
   116→
   117→def manual_clusters_with_issues(plan: dict) -> list[str]:
   118→    """Return names of non-auto clusters that have issues."""
   119→    return [
   120→        name for name, c in plan.get("clusters", {}).items()
   121→        if c.get("issue_ids") and not c.get("auto")
   122→    ]
   123→
   124→def apply_completion(
   125→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   126→    plan: dict,
   127→    strategy: str,
   128→    *,
   129→    services: TriageServices | None = None,
   130→) -> None:
   131→    """Shared completion logic: update meta, remove triage::pending, save."""
   132→    resolved_services = services or default_triage_services()
   133→    runtime = resolved_services.command_runtime(args)
   134→    state = runtime.state
   135→
   136→    organized, total, clusters = triage_coverage(
   137→        plan, open_review_ids=open_review_ids_from_state(state),
   138→    )
   139→
   140→    # Purge all triage stage IDs.
   141→    purge_ids(plan, list(TRIAGE_IDS))
   142→
   143→    current_hash = review_issue_snapshot_hash(state)
   144→
   145→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_triage_meta", {})
   146→    meta["issue_snapshot_hash"] = current_hash
   147→    open_ids = sorted(
   148→        fid for fid, f in state.get("issues", {}).items()
   149→        if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
   150→    )
   151→    meta["triaged_ids"] = open_ids
   152→    if strategy.strip().lower() != "same":
   153→        meta["strategy_summary"] = strategy
   154→    meta["trigger"] = "manual_triage"
   155→    meta["last_completed_at"] = utc_now()
   156→    # Archive stages before clearing so previous analysis is preserved
   157→    stages = meta.get("triage_stages", {})
   158→    if stages:
   159→        meta["last_triage"] = {
   160→            "completed_at": utc_now(),
   161→            "stages": {k: dict(v) for k, v in stages.items()},
   162→            "strategy": strategy if strategy.strip().lower() != "same" else meta.get("strategy_summary", ""),
   163→        }
   164→    meta["triage_stages"] = {}  # clear stages on completion
   165→    meta.pop("stage_refresh_required", None)
   166→    meta.pop("stage_snapshot_hash", None)
   167→
   168→    resolved_services.save_plan(plan)
   169→
   170→    cluster_count = len([c for c in clusters.values() if c.get("issue_ids")])
   171→    print(colorize(f"  Triage complete: {organized}/{total} issues in {cluster_count} cluster(s).", "green"))
   172→    effective_strategy = strategy if strategy.strip().lower() != "same" else meta.get("strategy_summary", "")
   173→    if effective_strategy:
   174→        print(colorize(f"  Strategy: {effective_strategy}", "cyan"))
   175→    print(colorize("  Run `desloppify next` to start implementation.", "green"))
   176→
   177→def find_cluster_for(fid: str, clusters: dict) -> str | None:
   178→    """Return the cluster name containing *fid*, or None."""
   179→    for name, c in clusters.items():
   180→        if fid in c.get("issue_ids", []):
   181→            return name
   182→    return None
   183→
   184→def count_log_activity_since(plan: dict, since: str) -> dict[str, int]:
   185→    """Count execution log entries by action since *since* timestamp."""
   186→    counts: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
   187→    for entry in plan.get("execution_log", []):
   188→        if entry.get("timestamp", "") >= since:
   189→            counts[entry.get("action", "unknown")] += 1
   190→    return dict(counts)
   191→
   192→__all__ = [
   193→    "apply_completion",
   194→    "cascade_clear_later_confirmations",
   195→    "count_log_activity_since",
   196→    "find_cluster_for",
   197→    "has_triage_in_queue",
   198→    "inject_triage_stages",
   199→    "manual_clusters_with_issues",
   200→    "observe_dimension_breakdown",
```

   245→            repo_root=repo_root,
   246→            output_file=output_file,
   247→            log_file=log_file,
   248→            deps=CodexBatchRunnerDeps(
   249→                timeout_seconds=batch_timeout_seconds,
   250→                subprocess_run=subprocess.run,
   251→                timeout_error=subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
   252→                safe_write_text_fn=safe_write_text,
   253→                use_popen_runner=(getattr(subprocess.run, "__module__", "") == "subprocess"),
   254→                subprocess_popen=subprocess.Popen,
   255→                live_log_interval_seconds=batch_live_log_interval_seconds,
   256→                stall_after_output_seconds=batch_stall_kill_seconds,
   257→                max_retries=batch_max_retries,
   258→                retry_backoff_seconds=batch_retry_backoff_seconds,
   259→            ),
   260→        ),
   261→        execute_batches_fn=execute_batches,
   262→        collect_batch_results_fn=_collect_batch_results,
   263→        print_failures_fn=print_failures,
   264→        print_failures_and_raise_fn=print_failures_and_raise,
   265→        merge_batch_results_fn=_merge_batch_results,
   266→        build_import_provenance_fn=build_batch_import_provenance,
   267→        do_import_fn=_do_import,
   268→        run_followup_scan_fn=lambda *, lang_name, scan_path: run_followup_scan(
   269→            lang_name=lang_name,
   270→            scan_path=scan_path,
   271→            deps=FollowupScanDeps(
   272→                project_root=runtime_project_root,
   273→                timeout_seconds=FOLLOWUP_SCAN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
   274→                python_executable=sys.executable,
   275→                subprocess_run=subprocess.run,
   276→                timeout_error=subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
   277→                colorize_fn=colorize,
   278→            ),
   279→        ),
   280→        safe_write_text_fn=safe_write_text,
   281→        colorize_fn=colorize,
   282→        project_root=runtime_project_root,
   283→        subagent_runs_dir=_subagent_runs_dir(),
   284→    )
   285→
   286→def _validate_run_dir(run_dir: Path) -> tuple[dict, Path, str]:
   287→    """Validate run directory, load summary, and return (summary, blind_packet_path, immutable_packet_path).
   288→
   289→    Raises CommandError on any validation failure.
   290→    """
   291→    if not run_dir.is_dir():
   292→        raise CommandError(f"run directory not found: {run_dir}", exit_code=1)
   293→
   294→    summary_path = run_dir / "run_summary.json"
   295→    if not summary_path.exists():
   296→        raise CommandError(f"no run_summary.json in {run_dir}", exit_code=1)
   297→    try:
   298→        summary = json.loads(summary_path.read_text())
   299→    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
   300→        raise CommandError(f"Error reading run summary: {exc}", exit_code=1) from exc
   301→
   302→    successful = summary.get("successful_batches", [])
   303→    blind_packet_path = Path(str(summary.get("blind_packet", "")))
   304→    immutable_packet_path = str(summary.get("immutable_packet", ""))
   305→
   306→    if not successful:
   307→        raise CommandError("no successful batches in run summary.", exit_code=1)
   308→    if not blind_packet_path.exists():
   309→        raise PacketValidationError(f"blind packet not found: {blind_packet_path}", exit_code=1)
   310→
   311→    try:
   312→        packet = json.loads(Path(immutable_packet_path).read_text())
   313→    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
   314→        raise PacketValidationError(f"Error reading immutable packet: {exc}", exit_code=1) from exc
   315→
   316→    summary["_packet"] = packet
   317→    return summary, blind_packet_path, immutable_packet_path
   318→
   319→
   320→def do_import_run(
   321→    run_dir_path: str,
   322→    state: dict,
   323→    lang,
   324→    state_file: str,
   325→    *,
   326→    config: dict | None = None,
   327→    allow_partial: bool = False,
   328→    scan_after_import: bool = False,
   329→    scan_path: str = ".",
   330→) -> None:
   331→    """Re-import results from a completed run directory.
   332→
   333→    Replays the merge+provenance+import step that normally runs at the end of
   334→    ``--run-batches``.  Useful when the original pipeline was interrupted (e.g.
   335→    broken pipe from background execution) but all batch results completed.
   336→    """
   337→    run_dir = Path(run_dir_path)
   338→    summary, blind_packet_path, _immutable_path = _validate_run_dir(run_dir)
   339→
   340→    runner = str(summary.get("runner", "codex"))
   341→    stamp = str(summary.get("run_stamp", ""))
   342→    successful = summary.get("successful_batches", [])
   343→    packet = summary.pop("_packet", {})
   344→    allowed_dims = {str(d) for d in packet.get("dimensions", []) if isinstance(d, str)}
   345→
   346→    # -- locate and parse raw batch results --
   347→    results_dir = run_dir / "results"
   348→    selected_indexes = [idx - 1 for idx in successful]  # convert 1-based to 0-based
   349→    output_files = {
   350→        idx: results_dir / f"batch-{idx + 1}.raw.txt"
   351→        for idx in selected_indexes
   352→    }
   353→
   354→    missing = [idx + 1 for idx in selected_indexes if not output_files[idx].exists()]
   355→    if missing:
   356→        raise CommandError(f"missing result files for batches: {missing}", exit_code=1)
   357→
   358→    batch_results, failures = collect_batch_results(
   359→        selected_indexes=selected_indexes,
   360→        failures=[],
   361→        output_files=output_files,
   362→        allowed_dims=allowed_dims,
   363→        extract_payload_fn=lambda raw: batch_core_mod.extract_json_payload(raw, log_fn=log),
   364→        normalize_result_fn=lambda payload, dims: batch_core_mod.normalize_batch_result(
   365→            payload,
   366→            dims,
   367→            max_batch_issues=max_batch_issues_for_dimension_count(len(dims)),
   368→            abstraction_sub_axes=ABSTRACTION_SUB_AXES,
   369→        ),
   370→    )
   371→
   372→    if not batch_results:
   373→        raise CommandError("no valid batch results could be parsed.", exit_code=1)
   374→
   375→    print(colorize(f"  Parsed {len(batch_results)} batch results from {run_dir}", "bold"))
   376→    if failures:
   377→        print(colorize(f"  Warning: {len(failures)} batches failed to parse: {[f + 1 for f in failures]}", "yellow"))
   378→
   379→    # -- merge --
   380→    merged = _merge_batch_results(batch_results)
   381→
   382→    # -- build provenance --
   383→    successful_indexes = [idx for idx in selected_indexes if idx not in set(failures)]
   384→    merged["provenance"] = build_batch_import_provenance(
   385→        runner=runner,
   386→        blind_packet_path=blind_packet_path,
   387→        run_stamp=stamp,
   388→        batch_indexes=successful_indexes,
   389→    )
   390→
   391→    # -- write merged output --
   392→    merged_path = run_dir / "holistic_issues_merged.json"
   393→    safe_write_text(merged_path, json.dumps(merged, indent=2) + "\n")
   394→    print(colorize(f"  Merged output: {merged_path}", "bold"))
   395→
   396→    # -- import with trusted source --
   397→    _do_import(
```

1→"""Stage-gate and coverage helpers for triage command handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     6→from desloppify.engine.plan import TRIAGE_IDS
     7→
     8→
     9→def _require_triage_pending(plan: dict, *, action: str) -> bool:
    10→    """Require at least one triage stage ID to be present in queue for an action."""
    11→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    12→    if order & TRIAGE_IDS:
    13→        return True
    14→    print(colorize(f"  No triage stage in the queue — nothing to {action}.", "yellow"))
    15→    return False
    16→
    17→
    18→def _validate_stage_report(
    19→    report: str | None,
    20→    *,
    21→    stage: str,
    22→    min_chars: int,
    23→    missing_guidance: list[str] | None = None,
    24→    short_guidance: list[str] | None = None,
    25→) -> str | None:
    26→    """Validate staged report presence/length and print consistent guidance."""
    27→    if not report:
    28→        print(colorize(f"  --report is required for --stage {stage}.", "red"))
    29→        for line in missing_guidance or []:
    30→            print(colorize(f"  {line}", "dim"))
    31→        return None
    32→    cleaned = report.strip()
    33→    if len(cleaned) < min_chars:
    34→        print(
    35→            colorize(
    36→                f"  Report too short: {len(cleaned)} chars (minimum {min_chars}).",
    37→                "red",
    38→            )
    39→        )
    40→        for line in short_guidance or []:
    41→            print(colorize(f"  {line}", "dim"))
    42→        return None
    43→    return cleaned
    44→
    45→
    46→def _triage_coverage(plan: dict) -> tuple[int, int, dict]:
    47→    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for triage progress."""
    48→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    49→    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
    50→    for cluster in clusters.values():
    51→        all_cluster_ids.update(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
    52→    queue_ids = [issue_id for issue_id in plan.get("queue_order", []) if issue_id not in TRIAGE_IDS]
    53→    organized = sum(1 for issue_id in queue_ids if issue_id in all_cluster_ids)
    54→    return organized, len(queue_ids), clusters
    55→
    56→
    57→def _unenriched_clusters(plan: dict) -> list[tuple[str, list[str]]]:
    58→    """Return clusters with issues that are missing required enrichment.
    59→
    60→    Requirements:
    61→    - Every cluster needs a description and at least one action_step.
    62→    - Small clusters (< 5 issues) need at least 1 action step per issue,
    63→      so each item has a concrete plan. Large clusters (>= 5) just need
    64→      steps overall (cluster-level plan is sufficient).
    65→    """
    66→    gaps: list[tuple[str, list[str]]] = []
    67→    for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
    68→        if not cluster.get("issue_ids"):
    69→            continue
    70→        if cluster.get("auto"):
    71→            continue
    72→        missing: list[str] = []
    73→        if not cluster.get("description"):
    74→            missing.append("description")
    75→        steps = cluster.get("action_steps") or []
    76→        issue_count = len(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
    77→        if not steps:
    78→            missing.append("action_steps")
    79→        elif issue_count < 5 and len(steps) < issue_count:
    80→            missing.append(
    81→                f"action_steps (have {len(steps)}, need >= {issue_count} for small cluster)"
    82→            )
    83→        if missing:
    84→            gaps.append((name, missing))
    85→    return gaps
    86→
    87→
    88→def _unclustered_review_issues(plan: dict, state: dict | None = None) -> list[str]:
    89→    """Return review issue IDs that aren't in any manual cluster.
    90→
    91→    When *state* is provided, uses open review/concerns issues from state
    92→    (the canonical source). Falls back to scanning queue_order for backwards
    93→    compatibility.
    94→    """
    95→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    96→    clustered_ids: set[str] = set()
    97→    for cluster in clusters.values():
    98→        if not cluster.get("auto"):
    99→            clustered_ids.update(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
   100→
   101→    if state is not None:
   102→        review_ids = [
   103→            fid for fid, f in state.get("issues", {}).items()
   104→            if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
   105→        ]
   106→    else:
   107→        review_ids = [
   108→            fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", [])
   109→            if not fid.startswith("triage::") and not fid.startswith("workflow::")
   110→            and (fid.startswith("review::") or fid.startswith("concerns::"))
   111→        ]
   112→
   113→    return [fid for fid in review_ids if fid not in clustered_ids]
   114→
```

1→"""Packet sanitization, hashing, and artifact layout helpers for review batches."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→from copy import deepcopy
     7→from datetime import UTC, datetime
     8→from hashlib import sha256
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→from typing import Any
    11→
    12→from desloppify.base.exception_sets import PacketValidationError
    13→
    14→_BLIND_PACKET_DROP_KEYS = {
    15→    "narrative",
    16→    "next_command",
    17→    "score_snapshot",
    18→    "strict_target",
    19→    "strict_target_progress",
    20→    "subjective_at_target",
    21→}
    22→
    23→_BLIND_CONFIG_SCORE_HINT_KEYS = {
    24→    "target_strict_score",
    25→    "strict_target_score",
    26→    "target_score",
    27→    "strict_score",
    28→    "objective_score",
    29→    "overall_score",
    30→    "verified_strict_score",
    31→}
    32→
    33→
    34→def write_packet_snapshot(
    35→    packet: dict[str, Any],
    36→    *,
    37→    stamp: str,
    38→    review_packet_dir: Path,
    39→    blind_path: Path,
    40→    safe_write_text_fn,
    41→) -> tuple[Path, Path]:
    42→    """Persist immutable and blind packet snapshots for runner workflows."""
    43→    review_packet_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    44→    packet_path = review_packet_dir / f"holistic_packet_{stamp}.json"
    45→    safe_write_text_fn(packet_path, json.dumps(packet, indent=2) + "\n")
    46→    blind_packet = _build_blind_packet(packet)
    47→    safe_write_text_fn(blind_path, json.dumps(blind_packet, indent=2) + "\n")
    48→    return packet_path, blind_path
    49→
    50→
    51→def _build_blind_packet(packet: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    52→    """Return a blind-review packet with score anchoring metadata removed."""
    53→    blind = deepcopy(packet)
    54→    for key in _BLIND_PACKET_DROP_KEYS:
    55→        blind.pop(key, None)
    56→
    57→    config = blind.get("config")
    58→    if isinstance(config, dict):
    59→        sanitized = _sanitize_blind_config(config)
    60→        if sanitized:
    61→            blind["config"] = sanitized
    62→        else:
    63→            blind.pop("config", None)
    64→    return blind
    65→
    66→
    67→def build_blind_packet(packet: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    68→    """Public wrapper for blind packet sanitization."""
    69→    return _build_blind_packet(packet)
    70→
    71→
    72→def run_stamp() -> str:
    73→    """Stable UTC run stamp for artifact paths."""
    74→    return datetime.now(UTC).strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
    75→
    76→
    77→def _sanitize_blind_config(config: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    78→    """Drop score/target hints from config while preserving unrelated options."""
    79→    sanitized: dict[str, Any] = {}
    80→    for key, value in config.items():
    81→        lowered = key.strip().lower()
    82→        if not lowered:
    83→            continue
    84→        if lowered in _BLIND_CONFIG_SCORE_HINT_KEYS:
    85→            continue
    86→        if "target" in lowered:
    87→            continue
    88→        if lowered.endswith("_score"):
    89→            continue
    90→        sanitized[key] = value
    91→    return sanitized
    92→
    93→
    94→def sha256_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
    95→    """Compute sha256 hex digest for path contents (or None on read failure)."""
    96→    try:
    97→        data = path.read_bytes()
    98→    except OSError:
    99→        return None
   100→    return sha256(data).hexdigest()
```

1→"""Holistic investigation batch builders for review preparation.
     2→
     3→Each batch builder returns exactly ONE batch with exactly ONE dimension.
     4→This ensures one prompt per dimension after explosion (which becomes a no-op).
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→
    11→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.models import HolisticContext
    12→
    13→_EXTENSIONLESS_FILENAMES = {
    14→    "makefile",
    15→    "dockerfile",
    16→    "readme",
    17→    "license",
    18→    "build",
    19→    "workspace",
    20→}
    21→
    22→
    23→def _normalize_file_path(value: object) -> str | None:
    24→    """Normalize/validate candidate file paths for batch payloads."""
    25→    if not isinstance(value, str):
    26→        return None
    27→    text = value.strip().strip(",'\"")
    28→    if not text or text in {".", ".."}:
    29→        return None
    30→    if text.endswith("/"):
    31→        return None
    32→
    33→    basename = Path(text).name
    34→    if not basename:
    35→        return None
    36→    if "." not in basename and basename.lower() not in _EXTENSIONLESS_FILENAMES:
    37→        return None
    38→    return text
    39→
    40→
    41→def _collect_unique_files(
    42→    sources: list[list[dict]],
    43→    key: str = "file",
    44→    *,
    45→    max_files: int | None = None,
    46→) -> list[str]:
    47→    """Collect unique file paths from multiple source lists."""
    48→    seen: set[str] = set()
    49→    out: list[str] = []
    50→    for src in sources:
    51→        for item in src:
    52→            f = _normalize_file_path(item.get(key, ""))
    53→            if f and f not in seen:
    54→                seen.add(f)
    55→                out.append(f)
    56→                if max_files is not None and len(out) >= max_files:
    57→                    return out
    58→    return out
    59→
    60→
    61→
    62→def _collect_files_from_batches(
    63→    batches: list[dict], *, max_files: int | None = None
    64→) -> list[str]:
    65→    """Collect unique file paths across batch payloads (preserving order)."""
    66→    seen: set[str] = set()
    67→    out: list[str] = []
    68→    for batch in batches:
    69→        for filepath in batch.get("files_to_read", []):
    70→            normalized = _normalize_file_path(filepath)
    71→            if not normalized:
    72→                continue
    73→            if normalized in seen:
    74→                continue
    75→            seen.add(normalized)
    76→            out.append(normalized)
    77→            if max_files is not None and len(out) >= max_files:
    78→                return out
    79→    return out
    80→
    81→
    82→def _representative_files_for_directory(
    83→    ctx: HolisticContext,
    84→    directory: str,
    85→    *,
    86→    max_files: int = 3,
    87→) -> list[str]:
    88→    """Map a directory-level signal to representative file paths."""
    89→    if not isinstance(directory, str) or not directory.strip():
    90→        return []
    91→
    92→    dir_key = directory.strip()
    93→    if dir_key in {".", "./"}:
    94→        normalized_dir = "."
    95→    else:
    96→        normalized_dir = f"{dir_key.rstrip('/')}/"
    97→
    98→    profiles = ctx.structure.get("directory_profiles", {})
    99→    profile = profiles.get(normalized_dir)
   100→    if not isinstance(profile, dict):
   101→        return []
   102→
   103→    out: list[str] = []
   104→    for filename in profile.get("files", []):
   105→        if not isinstance(filename, str) or not filename:
   106→            continue
   107→        filepath = (
   108→            filename
   109→            if normalized_dir == "."
   110→            else f"{normalized_dir.rstrip('/')}/{filename}"
   111→        )
   112→        normalized = _normalize_file_path(filepath)
   113→        if not normalized or normalized in out:
   114→            continue
   115→        out.append(normalized)
   116→        if len(out) >= max_files:
   117→            break
   118→    return out
   119→
   120→
   121→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   122→# Seed file collectors — shared across dimension batches
   123→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   124→
   125→def _arch_coupling_files(ctx: HolisticContext, *, max_files: int | None = None) -> list[str]:
   126→    """Files relevant to architecture/coupling dimensions."""
   127→    return _collect_unique_files(
   128→        [
   129→            ctx.architecture.get("god_modules", []),
   130→            ctx.coupling.get("module_level_io", []),
   131→            ctx.coupling.get("boundary_violations", []),
   132→            ctx.dependencies.get("deferred_import_density", []),
   133→        ],
   134→        max_files=max_files,
   135→    )
   136→
   137→
   138→def _conventions_files(ctx: HolisticContext, *, max_files: int | None = None) -> list[str]:
   139→    """Files relevant to conventions/errors dimensions."""
   140→    sibling = ctx.conventions.get("sibling_behavior", {})
   141→    outlier_files = [
   142→        {"file": o["file"]} for di in sibling.values() for o in di.get("outliers", [])
   143→    ]
   144→    error_dirs = ctx.errors.get("strategy_by_directory", {})
   145→    mixed_dir_files: list[dict[str, str]] = []
   146→    for directory, strategies in error_dirs.items():
   147→        if not isinstance(strategies, dict) or len(strategies) < 3:
   148→            continue
   149→        for filepath in _representative_files_for_directory(ctx, directory):
   150→            mixed_dir_files.append({"file": filepath})

## Detailed Findings: Desloppify Review Batch Runner - Codex Subprocess Execution Model

I've completed a thorough exploration of the desloppify review batch runner system. Here's the comprehensive end-to-end analysis:

### **1. THE EXACT CODEX SUBPROCESS COMMAND**

**Source:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_process.py:24-44`

The Codex subprocess command is built deterministically:

```python
def codex_batch_command(*, prompt: str, repo_root: Path, output_file: Path) -> list[str]:
    """Build one codex exec command line for a batch prompt."""
    effort = os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_CODEX_REASONING_EFFORT", "low").strip().lower()
    if effort not in {"low", "medium", "high", "xhigh"}:
        effort = "low"
    return [
        "codex",
        "exec",
        "--ephemeral",
        "-C",
        str(repo_root),
        "-s",
        "workspace-write",
        "-c",
        'approval_policy="never"',
        "-c",
        f'model_reasoning_effort="{effort}"',
        "-o",
        str(output_file),
        prompt,
    ]
```

**Key pattern:** The prompt text is passed as the final positional argument, and output is redirected to a file via `-o` flag. The working directory is set to the repo root via `-C`. Reasoning effort is configurable via `DESLOPPIFY_CODEX_REASONING_EFFORT` env var (defaults to "low").

---

### **2. SUBPROCESS EXECUTION: DUAL-PATH STRATEGY**

**Source:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/_runner_process_attempts.py:32-180`

The runner supports TWO execution modes, selected via `deps.use_popen_runner`:

#### **Path A: Popen (Live Streaming + Stall Detection)**
Used when `use_popen` is True:

```python
def _run_via_popen(
    cmd: list[str],
    deps: CodexBatchRunnerDeps,
    state: _RunnerState,
    ctx: _AttemptContext,
    interval: float,
    stall_seconds: int,
) -> _ExecutionResult:
    """Execute batch via Popen with live streaming and stall recovery."""
    writer_thread = _start_live_writer(state, ctx, interval)
    try:
        process = deps.subprocess_popen(
            cmd,
            stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
            stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
            text=True,
            bufsize=1,  # Line buffering for live streaming
        )
    except OSError as exc:
        state.stop_event.set()
        writer_thread.join(timeout=2)
        ctx.log_sections.append(f"{ctx.header}\n\nRUNNER ERROR:\n{exc}\n")
        ctx.safe_write_text_fn(ctx.log_file, "\n\n".join(ctx.log_sections))
        return _ExecutionResult(code=127, stdout_text="", stderr_text="", early_return=127)

    # Spawn two daemon threads to drain stdout/stderr independently
    stdout_thread = threading.Thread(
        target=_drain_stream,
        args=(process.stdout, state.stdout_chunks, state),
        daemon=True,
    )
    stderr_thread = threading.Thread(
        target=_drain_stream,
        args=(process.stderr, state.stderr_chunks, state),
        daemon=True,
    )
    stdout_thread.start()
    stderr_thread.start()

    # Main loop: poll for completion, check timeout, detect stall
    timed_out = False
    stalled = False
    recovered_from_stall = False
    output_signature: tuple[int, int] | None = None
    output_stable_since: float | None = None

    while process.poll() is None:  # While still running
        now_monotonic = time.monotonic()
        elapsed = int(max(0.0, now_monotonic - ctx.started_monotonic))
        
        # Timeout check
        if elapsed >= deps.timeout_seconds:
            with state.lock:
                state.runner_note = f"timeout after {deps.timeout_seconds}s"
            timed_out = True
            _terminate_process(process)
            break
            
        # Stall detection (output hasn't changed AND no stream activity)
        if stall_seconds > 0:
            with state.lock:
                last_activity = state.last_stream_activity
            stalled, output_signature, output_stable_since = _check_stall(
                ctx.output_file,
                output_signature,
                output_stable_since,
                now_monotonic,
                last_activity,
                stall_seconds,
            )
            if stalled:
                with state.lock:
                    state.runner_note = (
                        f"stall recovery triggered after {stall_seconds}s "
                        "with stable output state"
                    )
                recovered_from_stall = _output_file_has_json_payload(ctx.output_file)
                _terminate_process(process)
                break
        deps.sleep_fn(min(interval, 1.0))

    # Cleanup: join threads, write snapshot
    if process.poll() is None:
        _terminate_process(process)
    stdout_thread.join(timeout=2)
    stderr_thread.join(timeout=2)
    state.stop_event.set()
    writer_thread.join(timeout=2)
    _write_live_snapshot(state, ctx)

    return _ExecutionResult(
        code=int(process.returncode or 0),
        stdout_text="".join(state.stdout_chunks),
        stderr_text="".join(state.stderr_chunks),
        timed_out=timed_out,
        stalled=stalled,
        recovered_from_stall=recovered_from_stall,
    )
```

**Key mechanisms:**
- **Live streaming:** Two daemon threads (`stdout_thread`, `stderr_thread`) continuously drain output pipes into `state.stdout_chunks` and `state.stderr_chunks`
- **Activity tracking:** Each read updates `state.last_stream_activity` timestamp (thread-safe via lock)
- **Stall detection:** Compares file signature (size + mtime) against previous snapshot; if signature is stable AND no stream activity for `stall_seconds`, triggers early termination
- **Stall recovery:** Before terminating, checks if output file contains valid JSON payload—if yes, treats it as success (code 0)
- **Live log snapshots:** A background thread writes periodic snapshots to the log file every `interval` seconds

#### **Path B: subprocess.run (Simple Blocking)**
Used when Popen is not available:

```python
def _run_via_subprocess(
    cmd: list[str],
    deps: CodexBatchRunnerDeps,
    state: _RunnerState,
    ctx: _AttemptContext,
    interval: float,
) -> _ExecutionResult:
    """Execute batch via subprocess.run."""
    writer_thread = _start_live_writer(state, ctx, interval)
    try:
        result = deps.subprocess_run(
            cmd,
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
            timeout=deps.timeout_seconds,
        )
    except deps.timeout_error as exc:
        # ...timeout handling...
        return _ExecutionResult(code=124, stdout_text="", stderr_text="", early_return=124)
    # ... error handling ...
    return _ExecutionResult(
        code=int(result.returncode),
        stdout_text=result.stdout or "",
        stderr_text=result.stderr or "",
    )
```

**Simpler:** Just waits for subprocess to complete, no live streaming or stall detection.

---

### **3. STREAM DRAINING & ACTIVITY TRACKING**

**Source:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/_runner_process_io.py:107-126`

```python
def _drain_stream(stream, sink: list[str], state: _RunnerState) -> None:
    """Read lines from *stream* into *sink*, updating activity timestamp."""
    if stream is None:
        return
    try:
        for chunk in iter(stream.readline, ""):
            if not chunk:
                break
            with state.lock:
                sink.append(chunk)
                state.last_stream_activity = time.monotonic()
    except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
        with state.lock:
            sink.append(f"\n[stream read error: {exc}]\n")
    finally:
        try:
            stream.close()
        except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
            log_best_effort_failure(logger, "close review batch stream", exc)
```

**Key invariant:** Every line read triggers a lock-protected timestamp update. This allows the main thread to detect when the subprocess has gone silent.

---

### **4. STALL DETECTION LOGIC**

**Source:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/_runner_process_io.py:172-204`

```python
def _check_stall(
    output_file: Path,
    prev_sig: tuple[int, int] | None,
    prev_stable: float | None,
    now: float,
    last_activity: float,
    threshold: int,
) -> tuple[bool, tuple[int, int] | None, float | None]:
    """Check for runner stall. Returns (stalled, new_sig, new_stable_since)."""
    try:
        stat = output_file.stat()
        current_signature: tuple[int, int] | None = (
            int(stat.st_size),
            int(stat.st_mtime),
        )
    except OSError:
        current_signature = None
        
    if current_signature is None:
        baseline = prev_stable if isinstance(prev_stable, int | float) else now
        output_age = now - baseline
        stream_idle = now - last_activity
        if output_age >= threshold and stream_idle >= threshold:
            return True, None, baseline
        return False, None, baseline
        
    if current_signature != prev_sig:
        # Output changed, reset baseline
        return False, current_signature, now
        
    if prev_stable is None:
        return False, prev_sig, prev_stable
        
    # Output is stable; check both age and idle time
    output_age = now - prev_stable
    stream_idle = now - last_activity
    if output_age >= threshold and stream_idle >= threshold:
        return True, prev_sig, prev_stable
    return False, prev_sig, prev_stable
```

**Stall criteria:** BOTH conditions must be true:
1. Output file is stable (no size/mtime changes) for >= threshold seconds
2. No new data on stdout/stderr for >= threshold seconds

This prevents premature termination on naturally slow processes.

---

### **5. RETRY ORCHESTRATION**

**Source:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_process.py:47-122`

```python
def run_codex_batch(
    *,
    prompt: str,
    repo_root: Path,
    output_file: Path,
    log_file: Path,
    deps: CodexBatchRunnerDeps,
    codex_batch_command_fn=None,
) -> int:
    """Execute one codex batch and return a stable CLI-style status code."""
    if codex_batch_command_fn is None:
        codex_batch_command_fn = codex_batch_command
    cmd = codex_batch_command_fn(
        prompt=prompt,
        repo_root=repo_root,
        output_file=output_file,
    )
    config = _resolve_retry_config(deps)
    log_sections: list[str] = []

    for attempt in range(1, config.max_attempts + 1):
        header, result = _run_batch_attempt(
            cmd=cmd,
            deps=deps,
            output_file=output_file,
            log_file=log_file,
            log_sections=log_sections,
            attempt=attempt,
            max_attempts=config.max_attempts,
            use_popen=config.use_popen,
            live_log_interval=config.live_log_interval,
            stall_seconds=config.stall_seconds,
        )
        
        # Decision tree:
        early_return = _handle_early_attempt_return(result)
        if early_return is not None:
            return early_return
            
        timeout_or_stall = _handle_timeout_or_stall(
            header=header,
            result=result,
            deps=deps,
            output_file=output_file,
            log_file=log_file,
            log_sections=log_sections,
            stall_seconds=config.stall_seconds,
        )
        if timeout_or_stall is not None:
            return timeout_or_stall

        log_sections.append(
            f"{header}\n\nSTDOUT:\n{result.stdout_text}\n\nSTDERR:\n{result.stderr_text}\n"
        )

        success_code = _handle_successful_attempt(
            result=result,
            output_file=output_file,
            log_file=log_file,
            deps=deps,
            log_sections=log_sections,
        )
        if success_code is not None:
            return success_code
            
        failure_code = _handle_failed_attempt(
            result=result,
            deps=deps,
            attempt=attempt,
            max_attempts=config.max_attempts,
            retry_backoff_seconds=config.retry_backoff_seconds,
            log_file=log_file,
            log_sections=log_sections,
        )
        if failure_code is not None:
            return failure_code

    deps.safe_write_text_fn(log_file, "\n\n".join(log_sections))
    return 1
```

**Retry decision tree:**
1. If exit code is early-return-worthy (127, 1 from Popen creation), return immediately
2. If timeout/stall occurred, check if output file has valid JSON:
   - Yes → treat as success (code 0)
   - No → return 124 (timeout) or 124 (stall)
3. If exit code is 0 AND output file has valid JSON → return 0 (success)
4. If exit code is non-zero AND output contains transient failure marker → retry with exponential backoff: `delay = retry_backoff_seconds * (2 ** (attempt - 1))`
5. Otherwise, return failure code

---

### **6. PARALLEL EXECUTION ARCHITECTURE**

**Source:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_parallel.py:33-96`

```python
def execute_batches(
    *,
    tasks: dict[int, BatchTask],
    options: BatchExecutionOptions | None = None,
    progress_fn=None,
    error_log_fn=None,
) -> list[int]:
    """Run indexed tasks and return failed index list.

    Each value in *tasks* is a zero-arg callable returning an int exit code.
    All domain knowledge (files, prompts, etc.) is pre-bound by the caller.
    """
    resolved_options = _coerce_batch_execution_options(options)
    contract_cache: dict[int, str] = {}
    indexes = sorted(tasks)
    
    if resolved_options.run_parallel:
        max_workers, heartbeat = _resolve_parallel_runtime(
            indexes=indexes,
            max_parallel_workers=resolved_options.max_parallel_workers,
            heartbeat_seconds=resolved_options.heartbeat_seconds,
        )
        failures: set[int] = set()
        progress_failures: set[int] = set()
        started_at: dict[int, float] = {}
        lock = threading.Lock()
        
        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers) as executor:
            futures = _queue_parallel_tasks(
                executor=executor,
                indexes=indexes,
                tasks=tasks,
                progress_fn=progress_fn,
                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
                contract_cache=contract_cache,
                max_workers=max_workers,
                failures=failures,
                progress_failures=progress_failures,
                started_at=started_at,
                lock=lock,
                clock_fn=resolved_options.clock_fn,
            )
            pending = set(futures.keys())
            _drain_parallel_completions(
                pending=pending,
                futures=futures,
                heartbeat=heartbeat,
                indexes=indexes,
                progress_fn=progress_fn,
                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
                contract_cache=contract_cache,
                failures=failures,
                progress_failures=progress_failures,
                started_at=started_at,
                lock=lock,
                clock_fn=resolved_options.clock_fn,
            )
        return sorted(failures)
    return _execute_serial(
        tasks=tasks,
        indexes=indexes,
        progress_fn=progress_fn,
        error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
        clock_fn=resolved_options.clock_fn,
        contract_cache=contract_cache,
    )
```

**Concurrency model:**
- Uses `ThreadPoolExecutor` with configurable `max_workers` (min of batch count and configured limit, default 8)
- `_queue_parallel_tasks` submits all tasks immediately (via `executor.submit`)
- `_drain_parallel_completions` waits for completion via `as_completed()` with optional heartbeat timeout
- If heartbeat is enabled, emits progress every `heartbeat_seconds` seconds while tasks are running

---

### **7. BATCH TASK BINDING**

**Source:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batches_runtime.py:324-344`

```python
def build_batch_tasks(
    *,
    selected_indexes: list[int],
    prompt_files: dict[int, Path],
    output_files: dict[int, Path],
    log_files: dict[int, Path],
    project_root: Path,
    run_codex_batch_fn: Callable[..., int],
) -> dict[int, Callable[[], int]]:
    return {
        idx: partial(
            _run_batch_task,
            batch_index=idx,
            prompt_path=prompt_files[idx],
            output_path=output_files[idx],
            log_path=log_files[idx],
            project_root=project_root,
            run_codex_batch_fn=run_codex_batch_fn,
        )
        for idx in selected_indexes
    }


def _run_batch_task(
    *,
    batch_index: int,
    prompt_path: Path,
    output_path: Path,
    log_path: Path,
    project_root: Path,
    run_codex_batch_fn: Callable[..., int],
) -> int:
    try:
        prompt = prompt_path.read_text()
    except OSError as exc:
        raise RuntimeError(
            f"unable to read prompt for batch #{batch_index + 1}: {prompt_path}"
        ) from exc
    return run_codex_batch_fn(
        prompt=prompt,
        repo_root=project_root,
        output_file=output_path,
        log_file=log_path,
    )
```

**Pattern:** Each batch is a pre-bound `partial` callable that captures:
- Prompt file path (loaded at execution time)
- Output file path
- Log file path
- Project root
- The `run_codex_batch_fn` (which wraps retry logic and Codex invocation)

Tasks are zero-arg callables: `tasks[idx]()` → reads prompt, invokes Codex, returns exit code.

---

### **8. FULL ORCHESTRATION FLOW**

**Source:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/execution.py:391-746`

The `do_run_batches` function implements the complete pipeline:

```
1. LOAD/PREPARE PACKET
   └─ Load or freshly prepare blind packet (dimensions, batch metadata)

2. SELECT & VALIDATE BATCHES
   └─ Parse selected batch indices, validate coverage

3. PREPARE RUN ARTIFACTS
   └─ Create run_dir, logs_dir, prompts/, results/ directories
   └─ Write prompt files (one per batch index)
   └─ Build packet files

4. BUILD BATCH TASKS
   └─ For each batch, create a partial-bound callable that:
      ├─ Reads the prompt file
      ├─ Calls run_codex_batch(prompt, repo_root, output_file, log_file)
      └─ Returns exit code

5. EXECUTE_BATCHES (Serial or Parallel)
   ├─ Serial: for idx in indexes: code = tasks[idx]()
   │          if code != 0: failures.add(idx)
   │
   └─ Parallel: ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers) submits all tasks
              Waits via as_completed() with heartbeat
              Emits progress events (queued, start, done, heartbeat)

6. COLLECT & RECONCILE RESULTS
   └─ For each batch:
      ├─ Check if output_files[idx] exists
      ├─ Try to extract JSON payload
      ├─ If missing, parse from batch-N.log (STDOUT marker)
      ├─ Normalize via normalize_batch_result()
      └─ Mark as succeeded/failed/missing_output/parse_failed

7. MERGE BATCH RESULTS
   └─ merge_batch_results(batch_results)
   └─ Collect reviewed files
   └─ Write holistic_issues_merged.json

8. IMPORT & FINALIZE
   └─ do_import(merged_path, state, lang, state_file)
   └─ Optional: run_followup_scan() via subprocess
```

---

### **9. CONCURRENCY LIMITS & RUNTIME DEFAULTS**

**Source:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/_runner_parallel_execution.py:82-99`

```python
def _resolve_parallel_runtime(
    *,
    indexes: list[int],
    max_parallel_workers,
    heartbeat_seconds,
) -> tuple[int, float | None]:
    requested = (
        int(max_parallel_workers)
        if isinstance(max_parallel_workers, int) and max_parallel_workers > 0
        else 8
    )
    max_workers = max(1, min(len(indexes), requested))
    heartbeat = (
        float(heartbeat_seconds)
        if isinstance(heartbeat_seconds, int | float) and heartbeat_seconds > 0
        else None
    )
    return max_workers, heartbeat
```

**Defaults:**
- `max_parallel_workers`: 8 (capped by batch count)
- `heartbeat_seconds`: 15.0 (when run_parallel=True)
- `timeout_seconds`: 45 min per batch (configurable)
- `stall_after_output_seconds`: 90 sec (configurable)
- `max_retries`: 0 (configurable)
- `retry_backoff_seconds`: 0.0 (configurable)

---

### **10. RESULT COLLECTION & LOG EXTRACTION**

**Source:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_parallel.py:99-153`

Two-stage collection:

**Stage 1: Direct file read**
```python
if raw_path.exists():
    try:
        payload = extract_payload_fn(raw_path.read_text())
    except OSError as exc:
        logger.warning("Failed reading batch payload %s: %s", raw_path, exc)
        payload = None
```

**Stage 2: Fallback log parsing**
```python
if payload is None:
    payload = _extract_payload_from_log(idx, raw_path, extract_payload_fn)
    parsed_from_log = payload is not None
if payload is None:
    failure_set.add(idx)
    continue
if parsed_from_log:
    try:
        safe_write_text(raw_path, json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
    except OSError as exc:
        logger.warning("Failed writing normalized batch payload %s: %s", raw_path, exc)
```

**Log extraction logic** (`_runner_process_io.py:45-83`):
- Looks for `STDOUT:\n` marker in log file
- Extracts text between STDOUT and STDERR markers
- Calls `extract_fn` (JSON parser) on that section
- If no STDOUT section found, tries parsing entire log
- Prevents false positives by NOT falling back to full log if a concrete STDOUT section exists but is empty

---

### **KEY INSIGHTS**

1. **Subprocess Command Pattern:** Prompt is a string argument; output is a file; working directory is explicit. The codex binary expects JSON output to the file.

2. **Dual Execution Paths:** Popen for live monitoring + stall detection (complex but recoverable), subprocess.run for simple cases (no live streaming).

3. **Stall Recovery:** Timeouts/stalls are NOT automatic failures—if the output file contains valid JSON, the batch is treated as successful. This handles processes that go quiet but complete.

4. **Retry Strategy:** Transient failures detected via string matching in combined stdout/stderr. Exponential backoff with configurable base.

5. **Concurrency Model:** ThreadPoolExecutor-based, not process-based. Each thread runs one batch sequentially (Codex is subprocess-spawned, not a thread).

6. **Progress Model:** Progress events (queued, start, done, heartbeat) are emitted via callback; the executor is decoupled from progress reporting.

7. **Result Extraction:** Two-stage: direct file read, then log fallback. Normalization happens after both stages succeed. Failed batches are tracked but processing continues (with --allow-partial flag).

8. **Merge-First Architecture:** All batch results are merged deterministically BEFORE import, so a single unified payload is imported into state.

## Summary: Review Batch Prompt System

Based on my thorough exploration of the desloppify codebase, here's how the review batch prompt system works:

### Architecture Overview

The system uses a **multi-layer prompt construction** approach where holistic review packets are broken into dimension-focused batches, each with its own subagent prompt containing:

1. **Metadata & context** about the batch
2. **Dimension rubrics** (description, look_for, skip items)
3. **Scoring frame** and calibration guidance
4. **Navigation aids** (seed files, historical issues, mechanical signals)
5. **Task requirements** and output schema
6. **Holistic context** (blind packet) for exploration

---

## Key Prompt Components

### 1. **Prompt Sections** (`prompt_sections.py`)

The system renders prompts from composable sections:

- **Metadata block**: Repository root, blind packet path, batch name/rationale
- **Dimension prompts block**: Full rubric for each dimension (description + look_for + skip items)
- **Scoring frame**: Explains the judgment philosophy — score holistically, not formulaically
- **Scan evidence note**: Explains that `holistic_context.scan_evidence` is navigation, not evidence
- **Seed files block**: Starting point files specific to the batch
- **Historical focus**: Previously flagged issues (navigation aid, not evidence)
- **Mechanical concern signals**: Hypotheses from analysis (confirm/refute with code reading)
- **Task requirements**: Numbered items including dimension-specific guidance (e.g., abstraction_fitness → consult `holistic_context.abstractions` for delegation patterns, facade modules, TypedDict violations)
- **Scope enums**: Impact/fix scope vocabulary
- **Output schema**: JSON structure subagents must return

### 2. **Dimension-Specific Guidance** (`SCAN_EVIDENCE_FOCUS_BY_DIMENSION`)

Each dimension gets custom instructions on where to look in the holistic context:

```python
"initialization_coupling": "use evidence from holistic_context.scan_evidence.mutable_globals..."
"design_coherence": "use evidence from holistic_context.scan_evidence.signal_density..."
"error_consistency": "use evidence from holistic_context.errors.exception_hotspots..."
"cross_module_architecture": "consult holistic_context.coupling.boundary_violations..."
"convention_outlier": "consult holistic_context.conventions.duplicate_clusters..."
"package_organization": "ground scoring in holistic_context.structure (root_files fan_in/fan_out, directory_profiles, coupling_matrix)..."
"abstraction_fitness": "use evidence from holistic_context.abstractions (delegation_heavy_classes, facade_modules, typed_dict_violations, complexity_hotspots)..."
```

### 3. **Batch Structure** (`prepare_batches.py`)

Each batch contains:

- **`files_to_read`**: Seed files (collected from structural signals, god_modules, boundary violations, complexity hotspots, etc.)
- **`dimensions`**: The single dimension this batch evaluates
- **`name`**: Human-readable batch identifier
- **`rationale`**: Why this batch exists
- **`concern_signals`**: Mechanically-generated hypotheses (max 8 shown) with type, file, summary, question, evidence snippets
- **`historical_issue_focus`**: Previous issues for that dimension (up to 20 items) — marked with status and note
- **`_dimension_prompt`**: Inline prompt data after dimension explosion

### 4. **Blind Packet Structure**

The blind packet (sent to subagents) includes:

**Included:**
- `investigation_batches[]` — with `files_to_read`, `dimensions`, `concern_signals`, `historical_issue_focus`
- `dimension_prompts` — full rubric definitions
- `dimensions` — list of dimensions evaluated
- **`holistic_context`** — the core data structure for exploration:
  - `architecture`, `coupling`, `conventions`, `errors`, `abstractions`, `dependencies`, `testing`, `api_surface`
  - `structure`, `codebase_stats`, `authorization`, `ai_debt_signals`, `migration_signals`
  - **`scan_evidence`** — aggregated signals:
    - `mutable_globals`, `signal_density`, `exception_hotspots`, `boundary_violations`, `complexity_hotspots`, `duplicate_clusters`, `deferred_import_density`

**Excluded** (blind controls):
- `narrative`
- `next_command`
- `score_snapshot`
- `strict_target`, `strict_target_progress`, `subjective_at_target`
- Config keys containing score hints (`target_score`, `objective_score`, etc.)

---

## Output Schema Subagents Return

```json
{
  "batch": "batch_name",
  "batch_index": 1,
  "assessments": {
    "<dimension>": 85.5
  },
  "dimension_notes": {
    "<dimension>": {
      "evidence": ["specific code observations"],
      "impact_scope": "local|module|subsystem|codebase",
      "fix_scope": "single_edit|multi_file_refactor|architectural_change",
      "confidence": "high|medium|low",
      "issues_preventing_higher_score": "required when score > 85.0",
      "sub_axes": {"abstraction_leverage": 0-100, ...}  // for abstraction_fitness
    }
  },
  "issues": [{
    "dimension": "<dimension>",
    "identifier": "short_id",
    "summary": "one-line defect",
    "related_files": ["relative/path.py"],
    "evidence": ["specific code observation"],
    "suggestion": "concrete fix",
    "confidence": "high|medium|low",
    "impact_scope": "local|module|subsystem|codebase",
    "fix_scope": "single_edit|multi_file_refactor|architectural_change",
    "root_cause_cluster": "optional_cluster_name"
  }],
  "retrospective": {
    "root_causes": ["optional hypotheses"],
    "likely_symptoms": ["optional identifier list"],
    "possible_false_positives": ["optional prior concepts"]
  }
}
```

---

## System Prompt (Scoring Philosophy)

The **system_prompt** embedded in `dimensions.json` is extensive (see lines 384-end of dimensions.json). Key points:

- **Holistic judgment**: Score reflects "how well does code serve a developer on this dimension?"
- **Findings as illustration**: Issues explain WHY the score, not inputs to a formula
- **Evidence independence**: Signals/mechanical concerns are navigation aids, NOT evidence or confirmed issues
- **High confidence anchors**: Examples like "utils.py imported by 23/30 modules" and "getUser() mutates state"
- **Non-findings**: Consistent patterns (even if imperfect), trivial functions, framework boilerplate
- **Positive observation test**: Before emitting any finding, ask "does this need to change?" If no → reflect in score, not findings
- **Scores below 85 must have findings**: Every assessment <85 needs at least one defect finding or dimension_notes evidence
- **DEFECTS ONLY**: Never report positive observations; high assessment IS the positive signal

---

## Key Instructions to Subagents (from `render_task_requirements`)

1. Read the blind packet's `system_prompt` — it contains scoring rules
2. Start from seed files, then freely explore
3. Keep issues scoped to this batch's dimension
4. Respect scope controls (exclude, suppress, non-production zones)
5. Return 0-N issues (empty array allowed)
6. Dimension-specific focus (e.g., "for abstraction_fitness, use evidence from holistic_context.abstractions")
7. Do not edit repository files
8. Return ONLY valid JSON, no markdown fences

---

## Thorough Exploration: Desloppify Triage System

Based on my detailed analysis of the codebase, here are the complete findings on the desloppify triage system:

### 1. **Triage Stages Data Flow**

The system implements a **4-stage sequential workflow** defined in `TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS`:
- **observe** → **reflect** → **organize** → **enrich** → (commit/complete)

Each stage has explicit dependencies (in `triage_playbook.py`):
- observe: no prerequisites
- reflect: requires observe
- organize: requires reflect  
- enrich: requires organize
- commit: requires enrich

### 2. **Data Structures and Payloads**

**TriageInput** (collected before each stage, from `epic_triage_prompt.py`):
```python
- open_issues: dict[str, dict]           # All open review/concerns issues
- mechanical_issues: dict[str, dict]     # Non-review issues (for context)
- existing_epics: dict[str, Cluster]     # Current auto-triage clusters
- dimension_scores: dict[str, Any]       # Dimension scoring data
- new_since_last: set[str]              # Issue IDs new since last triage
- resolved_since_last: set[str]         # Issue IDs resolved since last
- previously_dismissed: list[str]        # IDs dismissed in prior triage
- triage_version: int                    # Next version number
- resolved_issues: dict[str, dict]       # Full objects of resolved IDs
- completed_clusters: list[dict]         # Clusters completed since last
```

**TriageStagePayload** (persisted for each stage, in `schema.py`):
```python
- stage: str                 # "observe", "reflect", "organize", "enrich"
- report: str                # Agent's analysis text
- cited_ids: list[str]       # Issue IDs mentioned in report
- timestamp: str             # ISO timestamp when recorded
- issue_count: int           # How many issues were analyzed
- recurring_dims: list[str]  # (reflect only) recurring dimension names
- confirmed_at: str          # ISO timestamp when user confirmed
- confirmed_text: str        # User's attestation text (80+ chars min)
```

**EpicTriageMeta** (top-level in plan.json):
```python
- triaged_ids: list[str]            # All IDs analyzed in current/last triage
- dismissed_ids: list[str]          # IDs dismissed permanently
- issue_snapshot_hash: str          # Hash of review issues at triage completion
- strategy_summary: str              # High-level plan narrative
- version: int                       # Triage version counter
- last_run: str                      # ISO timestamp of last triage
- last_completed_at: str             # ISO timestamp of last completion
- triage_stages: dict[str, TriageStagePayload]  # Current stage progress
- stage_snapshot_hash: str           # Hash of stage progress (for staleness detection)
- stage_refresh_required: bool       # Flag if issues changed during triage
- last_triage: LastTriageSnapshot    # Archived previous completion
```

### 3. **Triage Prompt Structure**

The prompt built by `build_triage_prompt(TriageInput)` contains these sections (in order):

1. **Existing Epics** — shows current auto-generated clusters with status, thesis, direction, and issue list
2. **New issues since last triage** — shows IDs and summaries of new review issues
3. **Resolved since last triage** — just lists IDs of resolved issues
4. **All open review issues** (main section) — formatted as:
   ```
   - [confidence] issue_id
     File: file_path
     Dimension: dimension_name
     Summary: summary_text
     Suggestion: recommendation_text
   ```
5. **Dimension scores for context** — shows dimension names with score%, strict%, and issue count
6. **Previously dismissed** — lists IDs to maintain unless contradicted

The **system prompt** (`_TRIAGE_SYSTEM_PROMPT` in `epic_triage_prompt.py`) instructs the LLM to:
- Cluster by ROOT CAUSE (not dimension)
- Provide thesis (imperative one-liner) for each cluster
- Order by dependency (what must be done first)
- Dismiss contradictory/nonsensical issues
- Mark which clusters are agent-safe vs need human judgment
- Return JSON with: `strategy_summary`, `epics[]`, `dismissed_issues[]`, `contradiction_notes[]`, `priority_rationale`

### 4. **Each Triage Stage: Consumption and Production**

**OBSERVE Stage** (`stage_flow_commands._cmd_stage_observe`):
- **Input**: TriageInput (full issue list, dimension scores)
- **Gate**: No citation requirement, just 50-100 char analysis of themes/root causes/contradictions
- **Output**: TriageStagePayload with:
  - report: the agent's analysis
  - cited_ids: extracted issue mentions (not gated, analytics only)
  - issue_count: count of open issues analyzed
- **Confirmation**: User must write 80+ char attestation mentioning at least one dimension from the issues

**REFLECT Stage** (`stage_flow_commands._cmd_stage_reflect`):
- **Input**: TriageInput + resolved_issues + previously completed clusters
- **Gate**: 50-100 char report + must mention at least one recurring dimension if recurring patterns detected
- **Recurring detection**: Calls `detect_recurring_patterns(open_issues, resolved_issues)` → returns `{dimension: {"open": [...], "resolved": [...]}}`
- **Output**: TriageStagePayload with:
  - report: strategy briefing
  - recurring_dims: list of dimension names with both open and resolved issues
  - issue_count: open issue count
- **Confirmation**: User writes 80+ char attestation referencing at least one dimension or cluster name

**ORGANIZE Stage** (`stage_flow_commands._cmd_stage_organize`):
- **Input**: Plan with manually created/enriched clusters
- **Gate**: Validates existing state:
  - At least 1 manual cluster with issues
  - All manual clusters have description + action_steps
  - Small clusters (<5 issues): need ≥1 step per issue
  - Large clusters (≥5): need ≥1 step total
  - All review issues must be in a manual cluster (or wontfixed)
- **Output**: TriageStagePayload with:
  - report: organization summary (50+ chars)
  - issue_count: count of manual clusters
- **Confirmation**: User writes 80+ char attestation mentioning at least one cluster name

**ENRICH Stage** (`stage_flow_commands._cmd_stage_enrich`):
- **Input**: Plan with existing clusters and action_steps
- **Gate**: All steps must have either:
  - detail: sub-points/paragraphs, OR
  - issue_refs: list of issue IDs addressed
- **Output**: TriageStagePayload with:
  - report: enrichment summary (50+ chars)
  - shallow_count: number of steps without detail/refs
- **Confirmation**: User writes 80+ char attestation mentioning at least one cluster name
- **Note**: User can still reorganize (add/remove clusters, reorder) at this stage

### 5. **Confirmation Flow**

Located in `confirmations.py`, each stage has a `_confirm_*` function:

1. **_validate_attestation(text, stage, dimensions, cluster_names)** → validates that attestation text references actual data:
   - observe: must mention at least 1 dimension
   - reflect: must mention at least 1 dimension OR cluster name
   - organize: must mention at least 1 cluster
   - enrich: must mention at least 1 cluster

2. **Cascade clearing**: When a stage is re-recorded, all later confirmations are cleared (via `cascade_clear_later_confirmations`), forcing re-confirmation of downstream stages

3. **Reuse logic**: If no new `--report` provided and an existing stage has a report, it's reused (with no cascade clear if previously confirmed)

### 6. **Cluster Management CLI Commands**

From `cluster_handlers.py`, the full dispatch table (`cmd_cluster_dispatch`):

| Command | Function | Args | Purpose |
|---------|----------|------|---------|
| **create** | `_cmd_cluster_create` | `<name>` `--description "..."` `--action "..."` `--priority N` `--steps-file` | Create new cluster |
| **add** | `_cmd_cluster_add` | `<name>` `<patterns...>` `--dry-run` | Add issues to cluster (supports glob patterns, file paths, detector names, hashes) |
| **remove** | `_cmd_cluster_remove` | `<name>` `<patterns...>` `--dry-run` | Remove issues from cluster |
| **delete** | `_cmd_cluster_delete` | `<name>` | Delete cluster (orphans issues) |
| **reorder** | `_cmd_cluster_reorder` | `<name>` `top\|bottom\|before\|after` `[target]` `--item PATTERN` | Reorder entire cluster or items within it |
| **show** | `_cmd_cluster_show` | `<name>` | Display cluster details (members, steps, commands) |
| **list** | `_cmd_cluster_list` | `--verbose` `--missing-steps` | List all clusters with metadata |
| **update** | `_cmd_cluster_update` | `<name>` `--description "..."` `--steps "s1" "s2"` `--steps-file` `--add-step "..."` `--detail "..."` `--update-step N` `--remove-step N` `--done-step N` `--undone-step N` `--priority N` | Update cluster description/steps/priority |
| **merge** | `_cmd_cluster_merge` | `<name1>` `<name2>` | Merge two clusters |
| **export** | `_cmd_cluster_export` | `<name>` `--format text\|yaml` | Export cluster steps to stdout |
| **import** | `_cmd_cluster_import` | `--file path.yaml` `--dry-run` | Bulk create/update clusters from YAML |

**Issue pattern resolution** (from `_resolve.py`):
- `f41b3eb7` — 8-char hash suffix
- `review::path::name` — full ID prefix
- `review` — all issues from detector
- `src/foo.py` — all issues in file
- `naming_attack` — last ::segment of ID
- `review::*naming*` — glob pattern
- `my-cluster` — cluster name (expands to members)

### 7. **Stage Record Mutations** (from `_stage_records.py`)

Each stage recording function mutates `plan["epic_triage_meta"]["triage_stages"]`:

- **_record_observe_stage**: Stores report, cited_ids, timestamp, issue_count
- **_record_organize_stage**: Stores report, cited_ids=[], timestamp, issue_count
- **_record_enrich_stage**: Stores report, timestamp, shallow_count
- **_record_confirm_existing_completion**: Special "confirmed-existing" path when re-confirming prior plan

Reusable reports logic: If no `--report` passed and existing stage has report, reuse it with `is_reuse=True` flag

### 8. **Display and User Guidance** (from `display.py`)

**Stage progress indicator** (`print_stage_progress`):
- Shows 4 stages with checkmarks, "needs confirm", or "current" status
- Displays enrichment gaps when in organize stage

**Action guidance** (`_print_action_guidance`):
- After observe: shows next-step guidance for reflect OR fast-track confirm-existing
- After reflect: shows dashboard of completed clusters and recurring patterns
- Before organize: shows cluster gaps or "create clusters" guidance
- Before enrich: shows shallow step warnings

**Coverage metric** (`triage_coverage`):
- Returns (organized, total, clusters) tuple
- Used to show "%  in clusters" progress

### 9. **Plan.json Structure**

```python
PlanModel {
    version: 8,
    created: ISO timestamp,
    updated: ISO timestamp,
    queue_order: [all issue IDs in priority order],
    clusters: {
        "cluster-name": {
            name: "cluster-name",
            description: str,
            issue_ids: [id1, id2, ...],
            action_steps: [
                {title: str, detail: str, issue_refs: [ids], done: bool}
            ],
            priority: int (optional),
            dependency_order: int (optional),
            auto: bool,
            status: str,
            triage_version: int,
            ...  # other metadata
        }
    },
    epic_triage_meta: EpicTriageMeta,
    skipped: {issue_id: SkipEntry},
    execution_log: [ExecutionLogEntry],
    ...
}
```

### 10. **Key CLI Entrypoints**

All in `/app/commands/plan/`:

- **cmd_plan_triage** (triage_handlers.py) — Main triage dispatcher
  - `--start` — inject triage stage IDs into queue
  - `--stage observe|reflect|organize|enrich --report "..."` — record stage
  - `--confirm observe|reflect|organize|enrich --attestation "..."` — confirm stage
  - `--complete --strategy "..."` — complete triage
  - `--confirm-existing --note "..." --strategy "..." --confirmed "..."` — fast-track re-confirm
  - `--dry-run` — build prompt without LLM call

- **cmd_cluster_dispatch** (cluster_handlers.py) — Routes to cluster subcommands
  - `desloppify plan cluster <subcommand> <args>`

### 11. **Validation and Gating**

From `_stage_validation.py` and stage_helpers:

**Observe validation**:
- Report length: 50 chars if ≤3 issues, else 100 chars minimum

**Reflect validation**:
- Report length: same as observe
- Must mention at least 1 recurring dimension if any detected
- Auto-confirms observe if attestation provided in same call

**Organize validation**:
- Manual clusters exist and have issues
- All clusters have description + sufficient action_steps
- All review issues clustered or wontfixed
- Reports 0-3 cluster operations logged since reflect (warning only)

**Enrich validation**:
- All steps have detail OR issue_refs (no bare steps)
- Auto-confirms organize if attestation provided

**Completion validation**:
- Organize stage must be confirmed
- Enrich stage must be confirmed
- Re-validates cluster enrichment to prevent plan.json direct edits
- At least some cluster coverage (no 0/{total})

### Summary

The triage system is a **multi-stage state machine** where:
1. Each stage consumes previously collected data (TriageInput)
2. Produces a recorded checkpoint (TriageStagePayload) in `epic_triage_meta.triage_stages`
3. Requires user attestation (80+ char text referencing actual data)
4. Cascades confirmation clears when re-recorded
5. Validates cluster enrichment before organization
6. Tracks stage progress, recurring patterns, and coverage metrics
7. Supports fast-track confirmation for stable plans with only additions
8. Logs all operations to `execution_log` with timestamps and actors

File locations (absolute paths):
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/epic_triage_prompt.py` — Data collection and prompt building
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/epic_triage.py` — Triage orchestration
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/` — All stage commands and display
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/cluster_handlers.py` — Cluster management commands
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema.py` — TypedDict definitions
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage_playbook.py` — Stage labels and canonical CLI commands
